#### K-169 · Claude's plan as a checklist
When Claude plans with TodoWrite, the transcript shows an "Update the plan" line for every change. The Claude Code app shows the list itself, ticked as it goes. The latest list lives in one place in the thread: a card of the card surface where the plan was first written, its items in order, done ones ticked and faint, the one in progress lit with its active form ("Running the tests"), pending ones plain, and "3 of 7 done" beside a small tally; each later TodoWrite updates that card in place, with the item that changed fading to its new state, and its call folds into the run with no line of its own. While a turn runs, the heads' main loop row (K-159) says the item in progress. It reads the stored tool calls, so a replayed thread shows its last plan, and it's drawn from TodoWrite's input, which another agent's plan tool could fill the same way. On Linear as REA-162.
Done when: a Swift test covers a plan written, updated and finished, and a replay; frames show the card through a plan's life; make test passes.
Notes: App/Plan.swift reads a TodoWrite's input into a Plan: its Todos in order, each with content, activeForm and a State of pending, inProgress or completed, and the done count worked out once. Plan.continues(into:) is the rule for a new card: a TodoWrite starts one when the thread has no plan, the last was all done, or fewer than half its items, matched on content, were in the last; otherwise it updates the last card wherever it is, and a new turn doesn't start one; an empty list clears the plan. Conversation.apply hands each TodoWrite's input to planned(_:at:): a list carrying the plan on is written onto the first call's ToolCall.plan in place, the way a workflow's run is on its call, and a new plan sets plan on its own call; Conversation.plan keeps the latest, so the plan is worked out once per update and never in a body, and a replayed thread rebuilds its last plan from the stored tool.use events with nothing new stored. TranscriptEntry.fold treats a call carrying a plan as a card that ends a run, like a workflow's, and skips any other TodoWrite, so an update has no line and the runs either side of it join. PlanCard (App/PlanCard.swift) is on the card surface: a 14pt GlassLevel filling in secondary ink beside "3 of 7 done", GlassLevel gaining a fill so a full plan doesn't turn amber and red, then each TodoLine, a faint check and faint words when done, WorkflowCard's lit Bead and the activeForm in primary ink while in progress, a faint Bead and secondary ink while pending; the card animates under Motion.fade on the plan's value, so an item that changes cross-fades. Conversation.planStep is the in-progress item's activeForm while a turn runs, which the heads' MainRow shows beside "Main loop". Checked: PlanTests has three, covering a plan written, updated and finished on one card with the update folded into its run, a lone update leaving a lone Read its line, and planStep through the turn; a second card after a finished plan and after a mostly different one, half carried on staying on the card, and an empty list clearing it; and a replay rebuilding the same items and plan with only user, tool.use, tool.result and turn.done stored. Frames of the real RootView offscreen, driven by routed events: the card written, an update cross-fading, the heads' main loop saying "Fixing the race in ReviewBook", five of five done, a second plan's card in the next turn, and both cards read back by a fresh AppModel. make test passes in a worktree with its Debug app renamed, 119 engine tests and 182 Swift tests. Not checked: a real TodoWrite from the CLI, the card among a real thread's text, Reduce Motion, and rule 6's timing of scrolling and streaming, not measured though the only per-body work added is one case in fold. A plan stopped with an item in progress still shows it lit after a replay, as the stored call says.

#### K-170 · Shortcuts you can change
Meriç: someone may want ⌘Return, not ⌥Return, to queue a message; and ⌘\\ doesn't suit a Turkish-QWERTY-PC keyboard. Settings › Shortcuts lists every shortcut, the list ⌘/ shows, and each can be given another key: the menu's own, the composer's Return, ⌥Return and ⇧Return, Esc's and the prompt's. A row records the next key pressed, says when it clashes with another and which, and has a way back to its default; the change holds across a relaunch and ⌘/ shows it. Menu items take theirs as their keyboard shortcut, so the menu shows them too. On Linear as REA-146.
Done when: reassigning New thread changes the menu's shortcut and survives a relaunch; reassigning the queue to ⌘Return makes ⌘Return queue during a turn and ⌥Return a new line; a clash is refused with its reason; unit tests cover storage and clash; make test passes.
Notes: App/Shortcuts.swift holds the store. A KeyCombo is a key, what it types on the keyboard's own layout with nothing held or a named key such as return, tab or delete, plus its modifiers kept as EventModifiers' raw value; it gives a KeyboardShortcut and a label in the menus' ⌃⌥⇧⌘ order, and reads a key-down through `characters(byApplyingModifiers: [])`, so ⇧⌘/ is / with shift. ShortcutAction lists the 18 menu items plus Send, Send After This Turn and New Line, each with its title and default. Shortcuts, AppModel.shortcuts, is an @Observable that keeps only what differs from the defaults, as JSON under `shortcuts`, so a default changed in a later version reaches anyone who never touched it. `set` refuses with the reason and changes nothing: "⌘N is New Thread", "⌘3 is Go to Thread 3", or "macOS keeps ⌘Q for Quit" for macOS's keys and the Edit menu's (⌘Q, ⌘H, ⌥⌘H, ⌘M, ⌘,, ⌘Tab, ⇧⌘Tab, ⌘`, ⌘Space, ⌃⌘F, ⌘Z, ⇧⌘Z, ⌘X, ⌘C, ⌘V, ⌘A); a menu's key needs ⌘ or ⌃ so it's never typed into the composer, and the composer's keys are Return with any modifiers. `restore` goes through `set`, so a default another action has taken meanwhile is refused too. `returnPress` makes the composer's one decision: the queue's key queues while there's a turn to wait for and is a new line otherwise, the send key sends, and any other Return is a new line, so plain Return is a new line once send has another key, and ⌘Return and ⌃Return, which used to send, are new lines unless given a job. Every Commands item takes `shortcuts.key(_:)`, and ⌘K's command rows, the send arrow's help and the help on the sidebar button, the capsule, the heads' mark and Add project take `label(_:)`. ShortcutList is rows that are an action or a fixed key, grouped as the menus are, OriCode, File, View, Thread and Help, then Composer and Review; the fixed ones are Settings, Go to Thread 1–9, the composer's Tab, ↑ ↓, ⌫, /, !, Return on a card, Esc and the review's keys. ⌘/ shows the keys as they are now, in a scroll view. Settings › Shortcuts lists the same groups under a line saying keys without a button don't move: each action's key is on a small ActionButtonStyle button which, clicked, reads "Type a shortcut…" while a local key-down monitor takes the next key, one a menu has included, until it's taken, Esc is pressed, the window stops being key or the pane goes; a refusal shows under the title and the row keeps listening. A Default button shows while a key differs, and Restore Defaults ends the pane. The recorder is a native Button and a key-down monitor, since neither SwiftUI nor AppKit has a shortcut recorder, and VoiceOver meets it as a button whose value is the key. Checked: ShortcutTests has nine: defaults and their labels, a change read back by a new Shortcuts on the same defaults as a relaunch would, a default stored as nothing, clashes, macOS's keys, the ⌘-or-⌃ and Return-only rules, ⌘Ş in ⌘\'s place, ⌘Return queuing once the queue has it while ⌥Return becomes a new line, plain Return a new line once send moves, and key-downs read into combinations. A local harness made the real Settings window key and sent it real events: a click on New Thread's button started recording, ⌘K was refused as "⌘K is Command Center" and ⌘Q as "macOS keeps ⌘Q for Quit", ⌥⌘T was taken, the main menu's New Thread then carried ⌥⌘T and no item ⌘N, the defaults held it and a new Shortcuts read it back, and Esc while recording left it there. In a RootView with a turn running and the queue on ⌘Return, "later" and ⌘Return queued it, and a, ⌥Return, b left "a\nb" in the field. make test passes in a worktree with its Debug app renamed, 119 engine tests and 188 Swift tests. Not checked: a real finger and menu bar, a Turkish layout, and VoiceOver on the recorder.
Commit: 49e5c55


## Exceptions

Times rule 1 was broken, with the reason. Keep this short.

- K-103: a drawer row is a List row that selects on a tap, not a Button: a Button in a row keeps the mouse, so the List's own drag to reorder never starts, and List's own selection would draw the system accent the brief rules out. VoiceOver meets each row as a button.
- K-59, K-65, K-77 and K-87: the model button opens a card of the app's own instead of a `Menu`: OriCode's mark as the picture of effort, an effort slider with a thumb where a `Slider` would do, a Fast circle and mode tiles, because Meriç wants the picker to look like the app and have some life in it, chose this one from three designs built for it, and a `Slider` can't carry the Default ring, the Ultracode stop or the mark in its thumb. The card is drawn on the window's glass like the slash menu, VoiceOver meets a native slider and a native segmented control, and the Thread menu keeps the native pickers for the keyboard.
